matrix. In contrast, we obtain a sample-optimal algorithm that does not require a bi-criteria guarantee. As noted above, our algorithm works for any distance matrix where the distance can be realized as a negative-type metric.

Theorem 4.28

(Sample-Optimal Negative-Type LRA)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a negative-type distance matrix. Given
\epsilon>0
and
k\in[n]
, there exists an algorithm that queries
\widetilde{O}(nk/\epsilon)
entries in
\mathbf{A}
and outputs matrices
\mathbf{M},\mathbf{N}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that with probability
99/100
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(1+\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}

Further, the algorithm runs in time
\widetilde{O}(n(k/\epsilon)^{\omega-1})
.

To demonstrate the connection between negative-type metrics and PSD matrices, we observe that a negative-type distance matrix
\mathbf{A}
can be realized as the distances corresponding to a point set
\mathcal{P}=\{x_{1},x_{2},\ldots x_{n}\}
such that
\mathbf{A}_{i,j}=\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{2}_{2}=\|x_{i}\|^{2}_{2}+\|x_{j}\|^{2}_{2}-2\langle x_{i},x_{j}\rangle
. Therefore, we can rewrite
\mathbf{A}
as
\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}-2\mathbf{B}
, where for all
j\in[n]
,
(\mathbf{R}_{1})_{i,j}=\|x_{i}\|^{2}_{2}
,
\mathbf{R}_{2}=\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{B}
is PSD. Further, we can obtain query access to
\mathbf{B}
by simply assuming w.l.o.g. that
x_{1}
is centered at the origin and the
i
-th entry in the first row corresponds to
\|x_{i}\|^{2}_{2}
. Therefore, we can simulate our PSD low-rank approximation algorithms on the matrix
\mathbf{B}
by only having query access to
\mathbf{A}
.

Our main contribution here is to show that if
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}
is an
(O(1),k/\epsilon)
-
SF
projection matrix for
\mathbf{B}
, then adjoining
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}
with the row span of
\mathbf{R}_{1}
and
\mathbf{R}_{2}
results in an
SF
-projection matrix for
\mathbf{A}
. Here, the row span of
\mathbf{R}_{1}
is
\mathbf{1}^{\top}/\sqrt{n}
and
\mathbf{R}_{2}
is
v
such that for all
i\in[n]

v_{i}=\|x_{i}\|^{2}_{2}/\sum_{i}\|x_{i}\|^{2}_{2}
. We note that once we obtain an
SF
projection for
\mathbf{A}
, we can run Algorithm
4.1
to output a
(1+\epsilon)
relative-error low-rank approximation.

Lemma 4.29

(Structured Projections for Distance Matrices)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a negative-type matrix such that
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}-2\mathbf{B}
, as defined above and let
\epsilon>0
. Given an
(O(1),k/\epsilon)
-
SF
projection
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top}
for
\mathbf{B}
, let
\mathbf{\Omega}^{\top}
be a basis for
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}
appended with the basis vectors for rowspan
(\mathbf{R}_{1})
and rowspan
(\mathbf{R}_{2})
. Then, with probability at least
99/100
,

\min_{\textrm{rank}(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{\Omega}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\top}\|^{2}_{2}\leq(1+\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}

Proof.

By Lemma 7 in
[
CW17
]
, for any symmetric matrices
\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Z}
such that
(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{Z}=0
and projection matrix
\mathbf{P}
, the following holds:

\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{Z}\|^{2}_{F}+\|\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}+2\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{Z})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{P}\right)

(4.44)

Applying Equation
4.44
with
\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{Z}=\mathbf{A}_{k}
, for any projection matrix
\mathbf{P}
, we have

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\|\mathbf{A}_{k}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}+2\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\right)

(4.45)

Next, we bound the
\|\mathbf{A}_{k}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}
as follows:

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}_{k}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}&\leq 2\|\mathbf{A}_{k}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\leq 2k\|\mathbf{A}_{k}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq 2k\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}\\
\end{split}

(4.46)

To bound the trace, we use the Von Neuman trace inequality,

\begin{split}2\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\right)&=2\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})^{2}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\right)\\
&\leq 2\sum_{i\in[n]}\sigma_{i}((\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}))\sigma_{i}((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P})\\
&\leq 2k\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|_{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|_{2}\\
&\leq 2k\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}\end{split}

(4.47)

It suffices to bound
\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}
for
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{\Omega}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\top}
. Since
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}-2\mathbf{B}
and
(\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2})(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})=0
, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}&\leq 2\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq 2\|\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\top})\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{B}_{k+2}\|^{2}_{F}\\
\end{split}

To relate
\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{B}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
back to
\mathbf{A}
, observe

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}-2\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}&=4\|\mathbf{B}-(\mathbf{R}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{A}_{k})/2\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\geq 4\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{B}_{k+2}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

Therefore,
\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\|^{2}_{2}\leq O(\epsilon/k)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
. We can thus bound Equations
4.47
and
4.46
with
O(\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
. Substituting this into Equation
4.45
, we conclude that
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{P}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(1+O(\epsilon))\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
, for
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{\Omega}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\top}
and the claim follows.
∎

Recall, we can compute an
SF
projection for the PSD matrix
\mathbf{B}
efficiently using Algorithm
4.3
and then solve the optimization problem in Lemma
4.29
using Algorithm
4.1
. We can therefore reduce low-rank approximation of negative-type matrices to PSD low-rank approximation with only
O(n)
additional queries and Theorem
4.28
follows.

4.5
Ridge Regression

We consider the following regression problem: given a PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
, a vector
y
and a ridge parameter
\lambda
,

\min_{x}\|\mathbf{A}x-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|x\|^{2}_{2}.

As a corollary of Theorem
4.1
, we obtain a faster algorithm for the aforementioned problem. We begin with the following simple lemma from
[
MW17
]
:

Lemma 4.30

(Lemma 26 in
[
MW17
]
)
.

Given a PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
, vector
y
, and
\lambda>0
, let
\mathbf{B}
be a matrix such that
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}\lambda
. Then, for any vector
\tilde{x}
such that

\|\mathbf{B}\tilde{x}-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|\tilde{x}\|^{2}_{2}\leq(1+\epsilon^{\prime})\left(\min_{x}\|\mathbf{B}x-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|x\|^{2}_{2}\right)

we have

\|\mathbf{A}\tilde{x}-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|\tilde{x}\|^{2}_{2}\leq(1+\epsilon^{\prime})(1+5\epsilon)\left(\min_{x}\|\mathbf{A}x-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|x\|^{2}_{2}\right)

Therefore, it suffices to find a rank-
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}\lambda
. Let
\widetilde{s}_{\lambda}
be an upper bound on the statistical dimension
s_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}^{2}+\lambda\mathbf{I})^{-1}\mathbf{A}^{2}\right)

Setting
k=\widetilde{s}_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}
, we can bound
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
as follows:

\begin{split}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\widetilde{s}_{\lambda}}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}&\leq\epsilon^{2}\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=k+1}\lambda_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})}{\sum^{n}_{i=1}\lambda_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})/(\lambda^{2}_{i}(\mathbf{A})+\lambda)}\\
&\leq\epsilon^{2}\frac{\sum^{n}_{i=k+1}\lambda_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})}{\sum^{n}_{i=k+1}\lambda_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})/(\lambda^{2}_{i}(\mathbf{A})+\lambda)}\\
&\leq c\epsilon^{2}\lambda\end{split}

We can then solve the regression problem
\min_{x}\|\mathbf{B}x-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|x\|^{2}_{2}
exactly in time
O(nk^{\omega-1})
and obtain the following result:

Theorem 4.31

(Ridge Regression)
.

Given a PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
, a regularization parameter
\lambda
and an upper bound
\widetilde{s}_{\lambda}
on the statistical dimension
s_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}^{2}+\lambda\mathbf{I})^{-1}\mathbf{A}^{2}\right)
, there exists an algorithm that queries
\widetilde{O}(n\widetilde{s}_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
entries of
\mathbf{A}
and with probability
99/100
outputs
\widehat{x}
such that for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\mathbf{A}\widehat{x}-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|\widehat{x}\|^{2}_{2}\leq(1+\epsilon)\left(\min_{x}\|\mathbf{A}x-y\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda\|x\|^{2}_{2}\right)

Further, the algorithm runs in
\widetilde{O}(n(\tilde{s}_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})^{\omega-1})
time.

Remark 4.32
.

Observe that we can derive a data structure from our algorithm that preserves the objective cost (up to
1+\epsilon
) for all
x
and
y
simultaneously and thus we obtain a coreset for Ridge Regression.

To complement the above algorithmic result, we present a new lower bound for coreset constructions for ridge regression, which matches our upper bound in all parameters. At a high level, our hard instance for constant
s_{\lambda}
consists of
1/\epsilon^{2}
blocks of all
1
s, each of size
\epsilon\sqrt{n}\times\epsilon\sqrt{n}
, placed randomly across the matrix. Since any coreset construction must preserve the cost of all
x,y
, we pick pairs
(x,y)
to be the eigenvectors of
\mathbf{A}
(scaled appropriately) and show that in order to preserve the cost of all pairs, the coreset algorithm must find all the blocks, which requires
\Omega(n/\epsilon^{2})
queries to
\mathbf{A}
. Repeating the above construction
s_{\lambda}
-times suffices to obtain a linear lower bound in terms of
s_{\lambda}
. Formally,

Theorem 4.33

(Coreset Lower Bound for Ridge Regression)
.

Given a PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
and
\epsilon,\lambda>0
let
s_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbf{A}^{2}+\lambda\mathbf{I})^{-1}\mathbf{A}^{2}\right)
denote the statistical dimension of
\mathbf{A}
. Then, any coreset construction
\mathcal{C}
that with constant probability, preserves the ridge regression cost up to
(1+\epsilon)
simultaneously for all
x,y
, must read
\Omega(ns_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
entries in
\mathbf{A}
.

We recall the lower bound instance for low rank approximation of PSD matrices shown by Musco and Woodruff :

Theorem 4.34

(Lower Bound for PSD LRA (
[
MW17
]
))
.

Given an
n\times n
PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
k_{0}\in[n]
, any randomized algorithm that outputs a rank
k_{0}
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that with probability at least
9/10
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(1+\epsilon_{0})\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k_{0}}\|^{2}_{F}

must query
\Omega(nk_{0}/\epsilon_{0})
entries in
\mathbf{A}
.

We consider the hard distribution defined by Musco and Woodruff, and show that we can obtain a low rank approximation to this instance with strengthened parameters by using a coreset for ridge regression.

Definition 4.35

(Hard Input Distribution for LRA (
[
MW17
]
))
.

Let
\mathbf{M}
be an
n\times n
matrix and let
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
k_{0}\in[n]
. Let
\gamma(n,\epsilon_{0},k_{0})
be a distribution over
\mathbf{M}
such that
\mathcal{S}\subset[n]
is a uniformly random subset of size
n/2
, which is further partitioned into subsets
\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathcal{S}_{2},\ldots\mathcal{S}_{k_{0}}
such that for all
\ell\in[k]
,
\mathcal{S}_{\ell}
is picked uniformly at random and
|\mathcal{S}_{\ell}|=n/(2k_{0})
. For each subset
\mathcal{S}_{\ell}
, let
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
denote the principle submatrix of
A
indexed by the set
\mathcal{S}_{\ell}
. Then, with probability
1/2
,
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
is such that all the diagonal entries are set to
1
and a uniformly random principle submatrix of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
, indexed by the set
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
, such that
|\mathcal{T}_{\ell}|=c\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}|\mathcal{S}_{\ell}|}
is set to all
1
s. With the remaining probability
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
is set to the
\mathbf{I}
.

We show that we can derive a low-rank matrix
\mathbf{B}
that satisfies the relative-error guarantee above from a coreset for ridge regression.

Proof of Theorem
4.33
.

We show a proof by contradiction, where the high level idea is that a coreset for ridge regression can be used to derive a low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{A}
, when
\mathbf{A}
is picked from
\gamma(n,O(1),s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
(the hard distribution defined in
4.35
). First, we observe that with probability at least
99/100
, the input distribution has
\Omega(s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
blocks that contain a principle submatrix with all
1
s. To see this let
X_{1},\ldots X_{s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}}
be indicators for the corresponding blocks
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
having a principle submatrix of all
1
s. Then,

{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{\ell\in[s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}]}X_{\ell}\right]=\frac{\epsilon^{2}n}{2s_{\lambda}}

(4.48)

Since the
X_{\ell}
’s are independent, by a Chernoff bound we have

\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\sum_{\ell\in[s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}]}X_{\ell}\leq(1-\delta)\frac{\epsilon^{2}n}{2s_{\lambda}}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{c\delta\epsilon^{2}n}{s_{\lambda}}\right)

(4.49)

For
n\geq\Omega(s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
, we can bound the above probability by
1/100
. We begin by showing that for our input instance,
s_{\lambda}=\Theta(n/\lambda)
and thus the aforementioned equations differ by
O(\epsilon n/s_{\lambda})
. To see this observe

s_{\lambda}=\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})}{\sigma_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})+\lambda}

(4.50)

Then, there are
s_{\lambda}/2\epsilon^{2}
large eigenvalues, each of magnitude
\epsilon\sqrt{n/s_{\lambda}}
and thus the total contribution is

\frac{s_{\lambda}}{2\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{(\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda})}{(\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda})+\lambda}=\frac{n}{\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda}+\lambda}

The remaining eigenvalues simply contribution
1/(1+\lambda)
to the sum and since there are at most
n
of them, the total contribution is
n/(1+\lambda)
. Therefore, we can conclude
s_{\lambda}=\Theta(n/\lambda)
.

For a block in
\mathbf{A}
indexed by
\ell
, let
\hat{x}_{\ell}
be the eigenvector supported on indices in
\mathcal{S}_{\ell}
and let
\hat{y}_{\ell}=\sqrt{n/s_{\lambda}}\hat{x}_{\ell}
. For non-identity blocks,
\mathbf{A}\hat{x}_{\ell}=|\mathcal{T}_{\ell}|\hat{x}_{\ell}=\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda}}\hat{x}_{\ell}
and the regression cost is

\|(1-\epsilon)\sqrt{n/s_{\lambda}}\hat{x}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda=(1-2\epsilon)n/s_{\lambda}+cn/s_{\lambda}

(4.51)

When the block indexed by
\ell
is the identity block, we get
\mathbf{A}\hat{x}_{\ell}=\hat{x}
and the regression cost is

\|(\sqrt{n/s_{\lambda}}-1)\hat{x}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{2}+\lambda=(n/s_{\lambda}+1-2\sqrt{n/s_{\lambda}})+cn/s_{\lambda}

(4.52)

Instead consider a vector that intersects an eigenvector
\tilde{x}_{\ell}
on a
(1-\gamma)
-fraction of the support and the rest is arbitrary. Then, when an an all
1
s block exists,
\mathbf{A}\tilde{x}_{\ell}\geq(1-\gamma)^{2}|\mathcal{T}_{\ell}|\hat{x}_{\ell}=(1-\gamma)^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda}}\hat{x}_{\ell}
and thus the regression cost is at most

(1-\epsilon(1-2\gamma))^{2}n/s_{\lambda}+cn/s_{\lambda}

Further, when the block is simply the identity, a similar calculation shows that the regression cost is at least
(1-2\epsilon\gamma)^{2}n/s_{\lambda}+cn/s_{\lambda}
. Therefore, the ridge regression cost determines the existence of a
(1-2\gamma)
-fraction of an all
1
s principle submatrix even when
\hat{x}_{\ell}
intersects with an eigenvector on a
(1-\gamma)
-fraction of coordinates.

Consider a coreset
\mathcal{C}
for the above instance. Since this coreset preserves the ridge regression objective upto a
(1+\epsilon/1000)
factor for all
x,y
, as per our above discussion we can query the coreset on the tuples
(\hat{x}_{\ell},\hat{y}_{\ell})
, which represent the eigenvectors of each block, to determine if a block contains a principle submatrix with all
1
s. However, a priori we do not know the support of the eigenvector within each block
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{S}_{\ell}}
.

Instead we query the coreset on all possible supports and show we can determine the right one as follows: let
\tilde{x}
be supported on a set that intersects with a principle submatrix of all
1
s on at most a
\gamma
fraction. Observe that
\mathbf{A}\tilde{x}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\gamma^{2}\epsilon\tilde{x}\right)
and thus the ridge regression cost can be lower bounded as follows:

(1-\epsilon\gamma)^{2}n/s_{\lambda}+cn/s_{\lambda}

(4.53)

We therefore take the set of all vectors on which the coreset cost is less than the above cost and let the resulting list be
\mathcal{L}
. Note, this list must include the eigenvectors and further, only includes vectors which intersect an all
1
’s submatrix on a
1-\gamma
-fraction. Therefore, picking a set of
\epsilon^{2}n/s_{\lambda}
vectors that have maximum support suffices.

Since we detect a
(1-\gamma)
-fraction of all principle submatrices in
\mathbf{A}
, it follows that we can output a
1+c^{\prime}
-approximate low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{A}
, for a fixed small constant
c^{\prime}
. To see this, observe that the optimal low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{A}
is given by the matrix that selects all the principle submatrices with all
1
s and thus
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k_{0}}\|^{2}_{F}=n-k_{0}=n-s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}
. Further, our approximation to
\mathbf{A}_{k}
, denoted by
\mathbf{B}
, matches
\mathbf{A}_{k}
on a
(1-\gamma)
-fraction of each principle submatrix of all
1
s and thus we match
\mathbf{A}_{k}
on these entries. Subsequently we bound the additional cost that
\mathbf{B}
incurs which
\mathbf{A}_{k}
does not. This includes entries that are
1
in
\mathbf{A}_{k}
and
0
in
\mathbf{B}
and vice versa.

To bound the cost of the entries that exist in
\mathbf{A}_{k}
but do not exist in
\mathbf{B}
, observe on each principle submatrix,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{A}
intersect in at least
(1-\gamma)^{2}
-fraction of the entries and thus the remaining entries are at most
(1-(1-\gamma)^{2})\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{2}n}{cs_{\lambda}}\cdot\frac{s_{\lambda}}{\epsilon^{2}}=4\gamma n/c
, since the size of each block is
\frac{\epsilon^{2}n}{cs_{\lambda}}
and the number of blocks are at most
\frac{s_{\lambda}}{\epsilon^{2}}
.
Finally, observe that since we do not pick exact eigenvectors we can have non-zero off diagonal entries in
\mathbf{B}
that do not exist in
\mathbf{A}_{k}
. However, we have at most
\gamma
-fraction of the support on each indicator vector remaining and contributing to two rectangular blocks of
1
s, each of size
\gamma\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{2}n}{cs_{\lambda}}\cdot\frac{s_{\lambda}}{\epsilon^{2}}=\gamma n/c
. Therefore, the additional non-zero entries in
\mathbf{B}
that do not appear in
\mathbf{A}
are
2\gamma n/c
in number.

Therefore, the overall cost
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(1+6\gamma/c)n
. By setting the constants
\gamma
and
c
and observing that
s_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2}\ll n
, we obtain a
(1+c^{\prime})
-low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{A}
, for any arbitrary small constant
c^{\prime}
.
Therefore, our reduction suffices to solve the hard instance above and a lower bound of
\Omega(nk_{0}/\epsilon_{0})=\Omega(ns_{\lambda}/\epsilon^{2})
queries follows.
∎

5
Robust Low-Rank Approximation

One drawback of relative-error guarantees is that the corresponding algorithms cannot tolerate any amount of noise. Therefore, we introduce a robust model for low-rank approximation by relaxing the requirement from relative-error guarantees to additive-error guarantees.
In the robustness model we consider, we begin with an
n\times n
PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
. An adversary is then allowed to arbitrarily corrupt
\mathbf{A}
by adding a corruption matrix
\mathbf{N}
such that the corruption in each row is an fixed constant times the
\ell^{2}_{2}
row norm of the row and the total corruption is an
\eta
-fraction of squared Frobenius norm of
\mathbf{A}
. While the adversary may corrupt any number of entries of
\mathbf{A}
, the norm of the corruption matrix is bounded and the algorithm has query access to
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
. We parameterize our lower bound and algorithms by the largest ratio between a diagonal entry of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, denoted by
\phi_{\max}=\max_{j\in[n]}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}/|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}|
. This captures the intuition that the diagonal is crucial for sublinear time low rank approximation and the sample complexity degrades as we corrupt larger diagonals entries.

5.1
Lower Bound for Robust PSD Low-Rank Approximation

In this subsection, we show a query lower bound of
\Omega(\eta^{2}n^{2}k/\epsilon^{2})=\Omega(\phi^{2}_{\max}nk/\epsilon)
for any algorithm that outputs a low rank approximation up to additive-error
(\epsilon+\eta)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Note, obtaining error smaller than
\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
is information-theoretically impossible and reflected in the query lower bound.

Our lower bound holds for randomized algorithms, and uses Yao’s minimax principle
[
Yao77
]
. The overall strategy is to demonstrate a lower bound for deterministic algorithms on a carefully chosen input distribution. We construct our input distributions as follows: let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a block diagonal matrix with such that
\mathbf{B}_{1}
is
5\epsilon/\eta\times 5\epsilon/\eta
randomly positioned, non-contiguous block with all entries
\sqrt{n\eta^{2}/5\epsilon}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}
is the identity matrix on the remaining indices.
\mathbf{A}
is clearly a PSD matrix since each principle submatrix is PSD. Observe
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=(25\epsilon/\eta^{2})\cdot(n\eta^{2}/5\epsilon)+(n-5\epsilon/\eta)=(1+5\epsilon)n-o(n)
. Further, the dense block
\mathbf{B}_{1}
contributes a total squared Frobenius norm of at least
4\epsilon n
and the diagonal entry contributes an
\eta/\epsilon
fraction of each row. Since
\epsilon\geq\eta
, the diagonal contributes at most the entire
\ell^{2}_{2}
norm.
The corresponding diagonal of
\mathbf{B}_{1}
also has
\ell^{2}_{2}
squared norm
5\epsilon/\eta\cdot n\eta^{2}/5\epsilon=\eta n
.

At a high level, the adversary can then corrupt the diagonal and set each diagonal entry to be
1
, making it hard for the algorithm to find rows corresponding to
\mathbf{B}_{1}
. We show that any
\epsilon
-additive-error low-rank approximation must detect at least one entry in
\mathbf{B}_{1}
to adaptively sample the corresponding row and column, but the diagonals no longer provide any useful information. Thus any algorithm must query most entries in
\mathbf{A}
. Further, in our construction, note
\phi_{\max}=\sqrt{n\eta^{2}/5\epsilon}
.

We first describe intuitively why a low rank approximation needs to recover many rows from the block
\mathbf{B}_{1}
. Since
\mathbf{A}
has this block structure, the best rank-
1
approximation satisfies
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}=n-|\mathbf{B}_{1}|
. Therefore, assuming the cardinality of
\mathbf{B}_{1}
is negligible, in order to obtain an overall error bound of
\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\epsilon n
, the algorithm must find a constant fraction of off-diagonal entries in
\mathbf{B}_{1}
. This is because
\mathbf{B}_{1}
contributes at least
5\epsilon n
norm. However, since the diagonals no longer convey any information about the off-diagonals, and the block
\mathbf{B}_{1}
is placed on a random subset of indices, any deterministic algorithm must read arbitrary off-diagonal entries until it finds a non-zero entry. Since there are only
25\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
non-zeros in
\mathbf{B}_{1}
, to find one in expectation (over the input distribution) requires sampling
\epsilon^{2}n^{2}/\eta^{2}
entries. While the above serves well as intuition, a rigorous proof requires many additional steps. We begin by defining a distribution over the input matrices :

Definition 5.1
.

Given
n\in\mathbb{N},\epsilon>\eta>0
, let
\mathcal{S}\subset[n]
be a uniformly random subset of size
\lceil 5\epsilon/\eta\rceil
. Let
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
be a distribution over matrices
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
such that
\forall i\in[n]
,
\mathbf{M}_{i,i}=1
and
\forall i,j\in\mathcal{S}
,
\mathbf{M}_{i,j}=\sqrt{\eta^{2}n/5\epsilon}
. All remaining entries in
\mathbf{M}
are
0
.

Next, we show that any
\mathbf{M}
sampled from
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
can be decomposed into
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that
\mathbf{A}
is PSD and
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. To see this, let
\mathbf{N}
be a diagonal matrix such that for all
i\in\mathcal{S}
,
\mathbf{N}_{i,i}=-\sqrt{\eta^{2}n/5\epsilon}
and let
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{N}
. We give an algebraic proof that
\mathbf{A}
is PSD, but
\mathbf{A}
can also be decomposed into a rank-
1
block of all
\sqrt{\eta^{2}n/5\epsilon}
-s corresponding to all
i,j\in\mathcal{S}
and identity on the remaining indices. For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\begin{split}x^{T}\mathbf{A}x&=\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{A}_{i,j}x_{i}x_{j}+\sum_{i,j\notin\mathcal{S}}\mathbf{A}_{i,j}x_{i}x_{j}\\
&=\sqrt{\eta^{2}n/5\epsilon}\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{S}}x_{i}x_{j}+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{S}}x_{i}^{2}\\
&=\sqrt{\eta^{2}n/5\epsilon}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}x_{i}\right)^{2}+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{S}}x_{i}^{2}\\
&\geq 0\end{split}

(5.1)

and thus
\mathbf{A}
is PSD. Further,
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=(25\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2})\cdot(n\eta^{2}/5\epsilon)+(n-5\epsilon/\eta)=(1+5\epsilon)n-5\epsilon/\eta
.
Then,
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}=5\epsilon/\eta\cdot\eta^{2}n/\epsilon=\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
, as desired. Intuitively, we show that if
\mathbf{B}
is a rank-
k
matrix that is a good low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{M}
sampled from
\mu
, then it cannot be a good low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{I}
.
To this end, we consider a distribution where
\mathbf{M}
is drawn from
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
with probability
1/2
and is
\mathbf{I}_{n\times n}
with probability
1/2
.

Definition 5.2
.

(Hard Distribution)
Given
n\in\mathbb{N},\epsilon>\eta>0
, let
\nu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
be a distribution over
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
such that with probability
1/2
,
\mathbf{M}
is sampled from
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
and with probability
1/2
,
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{I}_{n\times n}
.

We now show that a low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{M}
can be used as a certificate to separate the mixture
\nu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
since it can distinguish between the input being identity or far from it. Thus if the distributions are close in a statistical sense, any algorithm to distinguish between the two would require querying many entries in
\mathbf{M}
. Formally,

Lemma 5.3
.

(LRA as a Distinguisher.)

Let
\mathbf{M}
be a matrix drawn from
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
and let
\mathbf{B}
be a rank-
k
matrix that is the candidate low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{M}
such that
\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\epsilon n
. Then,
\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}\|^{2}_{F}>1.1\epsilon n
.

Proof.

Since
\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\epsilon n
,
\mathbf{B}
must have at least
4\epsilon n
mass on the off-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{M}
. So,
\mathbf{B}
must have at least
10\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
non-zero off-diagonal entries. Therefore, it must have at least
5\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
entries with squared mass
\epsilon n/2
. To see why, assume there is a subset of at least
12\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
entries, each being at most
\sqrt{n\eta^{2}/10\epsilon}
. Restricted to only these entries, the squared Frobenius norm difference between
\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{B}
is already at least
1.2\epsilon n
, contradicting our assumption.
Given that there exists a subset of
5\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}
off-diagonal entries having squared mass
1.2\epsilon n
,
\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{I}\|^{2}_{F}>1.2\epsilon n
, and thus
\mathbf{B}
is not an additive error low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{I}
.
∎

Theorem 5.4
.

(Lower bound for PSD Matrices.)

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a PSD matrix,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\epsilon>0
be any constant. Let
\mathbf{N}
be an arbitrary matrix such that
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Any randomized algorithm
\mathcal{A}
that only has query access to
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, with probability at least
2/3
, computes a rank-
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

must read
\Omega\left(\phi_{\max}^{2}nk/\epsilon\right)
entries of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
on some input, possibly adaptively, in expectation.

Proof.

Let Algorithm
\mathcal{A}
be a deterministic algorithm that outputs a rank-
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that it is an additive-error low-rank approximation
\mathbf{M}
. Let
T\subset[n^{2}]
be the subset of entries read by
\mathcal{A}
. Let
L(\mu)
denote the distribution of
T
conditioned on
\mathbf{M}\sim\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
and
L(i)
be the distribution of
T
conditioned on
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{I}
. By Lemma
5.3
, since the output of
\mathcal{A}
can be used to distinguish between the two distributions, it is well-known that the success probability over the randomness in
T
is at most
1/2+D_{TV}(L(\mu),L(i))/2

[
BY02
]
. Since we assume
\mathcal{A}
succeeds with probability at least

2/3
,

D_{TV}(L(\mu),L(i))\geq 1/3

(5.2)

It remains to upper bound
D_{TV}
in terms of
|T|
. Recall,
\mathcal{S}
is the random set of indices where
\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
is non-zero. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be the subset of
\mathcal{S}
restricted to the off-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{M}
. When
\mathbf{M}\sim\mu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
,
\forall i,j\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
,
\mathbf{M}_{i,j}
is non-zero and when
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{I}
, the same entries are
0
. Observe, for all
i,j\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
,
\mathbf{M}_{i,j}
are fixed. Further,
\mathcal{S}
is a uniform subset of
[n]
. Therefore,

\Pr\left[(i,j)\in T\mid(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}\right]=\frac{|T|\epsilon^{2}}{\eta^{2}n^{2}}

(5.3)

Then, with probability at least
1-|T|\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}n^{2}
,
\mathcal{A}
queries the same entries for both
L(\mu)
and
L(i)
.
Therefore

D_{TV}(L(\mu),L(i))\leq|T|\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}n^{2}.

Combined with Equation
5.2
, if
\mathcal{A}
succeeds with probability at least
2/3
,

|T|\epsilon^{2}/\eta^{2}n^{2}\geq 1/3

and thus
|T|=\Omega(\eta^{2}n^{2}/\epsilon^{2})
. Given that any deterministic algorithm must query
\Omega(\eta^{2}n^{2}/\epsilon^{2})=\Omega(\phi_{\max}^{2}n/\epsilon)
entries for
\nu(n,\epsilon,\eta)
, to now obtain a linear dependence on the rank
k
, we can use the standard approach of creating
k
disjoint copies of the block
\mathbf{B}_{1}
in the hard distribution, as shown in
[
MW17
]
.
The theorem follows from Yao’s minimax principle.
∎

5.2
Robust Sublinear Low-Rank Approximation Algorithms

In this subsection, we provide a robust algorithm for the model discussed above. We parameterize our algorithms and lower bound by the largest ratio between a diagonal entry of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, denoted by
\phi_{\max}=\max_{j\in[n]}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}/|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}|
. In addition, we provide robust
PCP
constructions, by introducing a new sampling procedure to construct projection-cost preserving sketches. Our sampling procedure is straightforward: we sample each column proportional to the diagonal entry in that column. This sampling requires
n
queries to the matrix
\mathbf{A}
to obtain an additive-error projection cost preservation guarantee.
Further, for the special case of correlation matrices, we can uniformly sample columns of
\mathbf{A}
to obtain a smaller matrix such that all rank
k
projections in the column and row space are preserved.

For our algorithms, we assume we know
\phi_{\max}
. In practice, this assumption may not hold, but we can query as many entries in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
as our budget allows, given that correctness holds only when the queries are at least
\widetilde{O}(\phi^{2}_{\max}nk/\epsilon)
. Since we read the diagonals of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and we know
\phi_{\max}
, we can obtain an upper bound on
\mathbf{A}_{i,i}
and
\mathbf{A}_{j,j}
. Therefore, whenever we query an off-diagonal entry in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, we can truncate it to
\phi_{\max}\sqrt{|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}|\cdot|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}|}
without increasing the corruption in our input.

Robust Projection-Cost Preserving Sketches.

Here, we show that diagonal sampling is a robust sampling procedure to create
projection-cost preserving sketches
.
We begin by relating the
\ell^{2}_{2}
row (or column) norms of a PSD matrix to it’s spectral norm. Let
\mathbf{A}
be a PSD matrix and let
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{U}^{T}
be the
SVD
for
\mathbf{A}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Given an
n\times n
PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
, for all
i\in[n]
,
\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{i,i}
.

Proof.

Observe,
\mathbf{A}_{i,*}=\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{U}^{T}
and
\mathbf{A}_{i,i}=(\mathbf{U}_{i,*}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{U}^{T})_{i}=\sum^{n}_{j=1}\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A})\mathbf{U}^{2}_{i,j}
. Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}=\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{i,*}=\mathbf{U}_{i,*}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{U}_{i,*}^{T}&=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma^{2}_{j}(\mathbf{A})\mathbf{U}_{i,j}^{2}\\
&\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}\mathbf{U}_{i,j}^{2}\\
&=\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\end{split}

∎

An immediate consequence of Lemma
5.5
is that the
\ell^{2}_{2}
norm of a row or column of a PSD matrix is at most
\frac{\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}}{\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}
. Note, this precludes matrices where most of the mass in concentrated on a small number of rows or columns.
Recall, we observe as input the matrix
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and our goal is to obtain a PCP for this in sublinear time and queries.

Musco and Musco
[
MM17
]
describe how to approximately compute the ridge leverage scores of
\mathbf{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}
(if
\mathbf{A}
is PSD) using a Nystrom approximation.
[
MW17
]
use this method to compute the ridge leverage scores of
\mathbf{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}
with
O(nk)
queries, where
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathbf{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. However, these approaches do not apply when we perturb the input and it may no longer be PSD. Therefore, the best known construction by Cohen et. al.
[
CMM17
]
would require
\Omega(nnz(\mathbf{A}))
time to compute approximate ridge-leverage scores of
\mathbf{A}
. Note, this does not use the structure that
\mathbf{A}
has.

In contrast, we show that sampling columns proporitional to the diagonal entries suffices to obtain a PCP. Note,
we only need to query the diagonal of
\mathbf{A}
to compute the distribution over columns exactly.
The main technical challenge here is to obtain the correct dependence on
n
and
k
and account for the perturbation to the input, given that our sampling probabilities are straightforward to compute and do not rely on spectral properties of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
. Note, the following is a structural result and while we do not know
\mathbf{A}
, we can still show the following :

Theorem 5.6
.

(Robust Spectral Bound.)
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an
n\times n
PSD matrix and
\mathbf{N}
be an arbitrary matrix such that
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
and for all
j\in[n]
,
\|\mathbf{N}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\leq c\|\mathbf{A}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}
, for any fixed constant
c
. Let
\phi_{\max}=\max_{j}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}/(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}
and let
q=\{q_{1},q_{2}\ldots q_{n}\}
be a distribution over the columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that for all
j
,
q_{j}=(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}/\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)
and let
t=O\left(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}\log(n/\delta)/\epsilon^{2}\right)
. Then, construct a sampling matrix
\mathbf{T}
that samples
t
columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that it samples column
(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j}
with probability
q_{j}
and scales it by
1/\sqrt{tq_{j}}
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, for any rank-
k
orthogonal projection
\mathbf{X}
,

\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}-\left(\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}\preceq\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\preceq\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}+\left(\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}

Proof.

First, we note that we cannot explicitly compute
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}
, but we can show that the sampling probabilities we have access to result in a PCP for
\mathbf{A}
.
Let
\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}
. For notational convenience let
\mathbf{A}_{j}=\mathbf{A}_{*,j}
. We can then write
\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{j\in[t]}\left(\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\mathbf{C}_{*,j}^{T}-\frac{1}{t}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in[t]}\mathbf{X}_{j}
, where
\mathbf{X}_{j}=\frac{1}{t}(\frac{1}{q_{j}}\mathbf{A}_{j}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{j}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T})
with probability
q_{j}
. We observe that
{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{X}_{j}]={\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\mathbf{C}_{*,j}^{T}-\frac{1}{t}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}]=0
, and therefore,
{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{Y}]=0
. Next, we bound the operator norm of
\mathbf{Y}
. To this end, we use the Matrix Bernstein inequality, which in turn requires a bound on the operator norm of
\mathbf{X}_{j}
and variance of
\mathbf{Y}
.
Recall,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{X}_{j}\|_{2}&=\left\|\frac{1}{tq_{j}}\mathbf{A}_{j}\mathbf{A}_{j}-\frac{1}{t}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}{t(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\|\mathbf{A}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{1}{t}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)+|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{N}\right)|}{t(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\left((1+\eta)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{c(\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)+|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{N}\right)|)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\phi_{\max}}{t}\\
&\leq\frac{c\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}}{t}\end{split}

(5.4)

where we use triangle inequality for operator norm to obtain the first inequality, triangle inequality up to a factor of
2
for
\ell^{2}_{2}
norms for the second inequality,
\|\mathbf{N}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\eta\|\mathbf{A}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}
and
\|\mathbf{A}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{j,j}
(from Lemma
5.5
) for the third inequality and definition of
\phi_{\max}
and
\eta=O(1)
for the fourth. Finally, we relate the trace of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{N}
to their respective Frobenius norm using Cauchy-Schwarz:

\begin{split}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{A})\leq\sqrt{\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sigma_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{A})\cdot n}=\sqrt{n\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}|\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{N}\right)|=\left|\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{A})\right|\leq\sqrt{\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sigma_{i}^{2}(\mathbf{N})\cdot n}=\sqrt{n\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}}\leq\sqrt{n\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\end{split}

where the last inequality follows from
\|\mathbf{N}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
.
Next, we bound
\mbox{\bf Var}[\mathbf{Y}]\leq{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{Y}^{2}\right]
.

\begin{split}{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{Y}^{2}\right]&=t{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\left((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}^{T}-\frac{1}{t}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)^{2}\right]\\
&=t{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\left((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}^{T}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)^{2}-\frac{2}{t}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{*,j}^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{t}\left(\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{(\mathbf{A}_{j}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{j})^{2}}{q_{j}}+(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T})^{2}-\sum_{j\in[n]}2\mathbf{A}_{j}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{j}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)\\
&\preceq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}{t\mathbf{A}_{j,j}}\left(\sum_{j\in[n]}(\mathbf{A}_{j}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{j})^{2}\right)\\
&\preceq\frac{c\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}}{t}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\\
\end{split}

(5.5)

where we use linearity of expectation,
(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T})^{2}\succeq 0
and
\|\mathbf{A}_{j,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{j,j}
.
Applying the Matrix Bernstein inequality,

\begin{split}\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{2}\geq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\right]&\leq 2n\exp{\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4}_{F}}{\frac{c\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{3}_{2}}{t}+\frac{2\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\left(\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\right)}{3t}}\right)}\\
&\leq 2n\exp{\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}t}{c\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}}\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4}_{F}}{\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{2}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}\right)\right)}\\
&\leq 2n\exp{\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}t}{c^{\prime}\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}}\right)}\\
&\leq\delta/2\end{split}

where the last inequality follows from setting
t=O(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\log(n/\delta)/\epsilon^{2})
. To yield the claim, we set
\epsilon=\epsilon/k
.
∎

We use the above spectral bound to show that sampling proportional to diagonal entries preserves the projection cost of the columns of
\mathbf{A}
on to any
k
-dimensional subspace up to an additive
(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
.

Theorem 5.7
.

(Column Projection-Cost Preservation.)

Given
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, where
\mathbf{A}
is an
n\times n
PSD matrix and
\mathbf{N}
is an arbitrary noise matrix as defined above,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\epsilon>\eta>0
, let
q=\{q_{1},q_{2}\ldots q_{n}\}
be a probability distribution over the columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that
q_{j}=\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Let
t=O\left(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}\log(\frac{n}{\delta})/\epsilon^{2}\right)
. Then, construct
\mathbf{C}
using
t
columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and set each one to
\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j}}{\sqrt{tq_{j}}}
with probability
q_{j}
. With probability at least
1-c
, for any rank-
k
orthogonal projection
\mathbf{X}
,

\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\pm(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

for a fixed constant
c
.

Proof.

Here, the matrix
\mathbf{C}
is actually a matrix we can compute. Observe that we can relate
\mathbf{C}
to the sampling matrix
\mathbf{T}
as defined in Theorem
5.6
as
\mathbf{C}=(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})\mathbf{T}
.
We follow the proof strategy of the relative error guarantees in
[
CMM17
]
and additive error guarantees in
[
BW18
]
but note, our spectral bounds from Theorem
5.6
apply to matrices that we do not actually compute.
Observe,
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)
. Then,

\begin{split}\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\end{split}

(5.6)

where we used the fact that for any projection matrix
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{X}^{2}
in addition to the cyclic property of the trace.
Similarly,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\end{split}

(5.7)

We first relate
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}\right)
. Recall,

{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}\right)\right]={\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\right)+2\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

Using a scalar Chernoff bound, we show that with probability at least
1-1/\textrm{poly}(n)
,
\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}=(1\pm\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. This is equivalent to
|\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. Observe, for all
j\in[t]
,
\mathbf{C}_{*,j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{q_{j^{\prime}}t}}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j^{\prime}}
for some
j^{\prime}\in[n]
. Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{q_{j^{\prime}}t}\|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}&=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)\epsilon^{2}}{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k\log(n/\delta)(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{c\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{n}\log(n/\delta)}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{k\log(n/\delta)}\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.8)

where we use
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
,
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{N}\right)\leq\sqrt{\eta n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
and
t=O(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k\log(n/\delta)/\epsilon^{2})
.
Therefore,
\frac{k\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\in[0,1]
. By a Chernoff bound,

\begin{split}\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\geq(1+2\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\right]&=\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\frac{k\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}}\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\frac{k\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}(1+\epsilon)\right]\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{k\epsilon^{2}\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\end{split}

(5.9)

We can repeat the above argument to lower bound
\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}
.
Therefore, with probability
1-\delta
, we have

|\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}

Here, we can upper bound this by observing
\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\|^{2}_{F}+\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}+2\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{N}\rangle\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}+3\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Therefore,

|\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}+(1+\epsilon)\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(\epsilon+2\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.10)

Next, we relate
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
. First, we observe

\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{T}=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}+(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}+(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}+(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}

(5.11)

We begin by first bounding
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
.
Observe,
\mathbf{X}
is a rank
k
projection matrix and we can represent it as
\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}
, where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and has orthonormal columns. By the cyclic property of the trace, we have

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{Z}\right)=\sum_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}

Similarly,
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}
. By Theorem
5.6
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in[k]}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}-\left(\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{I}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\right)&\leq\sum_{j\in[k]}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in[k]}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}+\left(\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{I}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\right)\end{split}

(5.12)

Since
\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}=1
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{Z}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
, we have

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}

(5.13)

Next, we focus on
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)=\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}\|^{2}_{F}
. Observe, since
\mathbf{T}
is an unbiased estimator of Frobenius norm, by Markov’s inequality we can show with probability at least
1-c
,
\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}\|_{F}=c\|\mathbf{N}\|_{F}=O(\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
. Therefore, we can upper bound
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
by
O(\eta)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Now, we focus on the cross terms. By Cauchy-Schwartz, and a Markov bound, with probability at least
1-c
,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\leq\|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\|_{F}\cdot\|\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}\|_{F}\leq O(\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.14)

Combining equations
5.10
,
5.13
,
5.14
and union bounding over the success of the random events, with probability
1-c
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}-O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}+O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

∎

Robust Row Projection Cost Preserving Sketches.
We now extend the diagonal sampling algorithm to construct a row projection cost preserving sketch for the matrix
\mathbf{C}
. We note that following the construction for
\mathbf{A}
does not immediately give a row PCP for
\mathbf{C}
since
\mathbf{C}
is no longer PSD or even square matrix. Here, all previous approaches to construct a PCP with sublinear queries hit a roadblock, since the matrix
\mathbf{C}
need not have any well defined structure apart from being a scaled subset of the columns of a PSD matrix. However, we show that sampling rows of
\mathbf{C}
proportional to the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{A}
results in a row PCP.

We begin by relating the row norms of
\mathbf{C}
to the row norms of
\mathbf{A}
. Note, we do not expect to obtain concentration here, since such a sampling procedure would then help us estimate row norms of
\mathbf{A}
up to a constant and we would be done by using
[
FKV04
]
. Therefore, we obtain the following one-sided guarantee:

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times t}
be a column projection-cost preserving sketch for
\mathbf{A}
as described in Theorem
5.6
.
For all
i\in[n]
, with probability at least
1-1/n^{c}
,

\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq O\left(\log(n)\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right\}\right)

where
c
is a fixed constant.

Proof.

Observe that
\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}=\sum_{j\in[t]}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,j}^{2}
, where
(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,j}^{2}=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}{t(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\mathbf{A}_{i,j}^{2}

with probability
\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Then,

{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}]=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{A}^{2}_{i,j}=\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
. Next, we compute the variance of
\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
.
\mbox{\bf Var}\left[\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]=t\mbox{\bf Var}\left[(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{2}_{i,j}\right]\leq{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{4}_{i,j}\right]
. Then,

\begin{split}t{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{4}_{i,j}\right]=\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{1}{tq_{j}}\mathbf{A}_{i,j}^{4}&\leq\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}{t(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\mathbf{A}_{i,j}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}\\
&\leq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)\phi_{\max}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2}\hskip 28.90755pt\textrm{ [AM-GM]}\\
\end{split}

where we use
\mathbf{A}^{2}_{i,j}\leq\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}
, which follows from applying Cauchy-Schwarz to
\langle\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{i,*},\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{j,*}\rangle
, i.e.,

\mathbf{A}^{2}_{i,j}=\langle\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{i,*},\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{j,*}\rangle^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{j,*}\|^{2}_{2}=\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}

Similarly, we bound

(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{2}_{i,j}=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}{t(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\mathbf{A}^{2}_{i,j}\leq\frac{2\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}}{t}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}

Applying Bernstein’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[|\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\gamma\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right\}\right]\\
&\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2},\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}{\left(\frac{2\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2}+\gamma\max\left\{\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}}{t},\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\\
&\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2},\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}{c^{\prime}\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}+c^{\prime}\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2}}\right)\\
\end{split}

where
\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right)^{2}+\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2}
follows from the AM-GM inequality. Setting
\gamma=\Omega(\log(n))
completes the proof.

∎

To construct a row projection cost preserving sketch of
\mathbf{C}
, we sample
t
rows of
\mathbf{C}
proportional to the corresponding diagonal entries of
\mathbf{A}
. Formally, we consider a probability distribution,
p=\{p_{1},p_{2},\ldots p_{n}\}
, over the rows of
\mathbf{C}
such that
p_{i}=\frac{\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}
. Let
\mathbf{R}
be a
t\times t
matrix where each row of
\mathbf{R}
is set to
\frac{1}{\sqrt{tp_{i}}}\mathbf{C}_{i,*}
with probability
p_{i}
. As before,
\mathbf{R}
can be represented as
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})
. We first obtain a spectral guarantee for
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}
, while we cannot actually compute this.

Theorem 5.9
.

(Spectral Bounds.)
Let
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}
be an
n\times t
matrix constructed as shown in Theorem
5.6
. Let
p=\{p_{1},p_{2}\ldots p_{n}\}
be a probability distribution over the rows of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}
such that
p_{i}=\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Let
t=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\right)
. Construct a sampling matrix
\mathbf{S}
that samples
t
rows of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}
such that row
(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}
is picked with with probability
p_{i}
and scaled by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{tp_{i}}}
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})-\frac{\epsilon}{k}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}\preceq(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\preceq(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{Y}=(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})-(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})
. For notational
convenience let
(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i,*}}
and
(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}=(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i,*}}
. We can then write
\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{i\in[t]}\left((\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{{i}}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i}}-\frac{1}{t}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\right)=\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{X}_{i}
, where
\mathbf{X}_{i}=\frac{1}{t}\left(\frac{1}{p_{i}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}-(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\right)
with probability
p_{i}
. We
observe that
{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]={\bf\mathbb{E}}[(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}-\frac{1}{t}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})]=\sum_{i}\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}-(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})=0
, and therefore,
{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{Y}]=0
. Next,
we bound the operator norm of
\mathbf{Y}
. To this end,
we use the Matrix Bernstein inequality, which in turn requires a bound on the operator norm of

\mathbf{X}_{i}
and variance of
\mathbf{Y}
.
Recall, for some
i^{\prime}\in[n]

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2}&=\left\|\frac{1}{tp_{i^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{{i^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i^{\prime}}}-\frac{1}{t}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{tp_{i^{\prime}}}\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{{i^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i^{\prime}}}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{t}\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|_{2}\\
&=\frac{\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{{i^{\prime}}}\|^{2}_{2}}{tp_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|^{2}_{2}}{t}\\
&\leq\frac{\log(n)}{tp_{i^{\prime}}}\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},*}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i,i}}{t}\right\}+\frac{\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|^{2}_{2}}{t}\hskip 36.135pt\textrm{[by Lemma \ref{lem:row_norm_bound}]}\\
&\leq\frac{\log(n)}{t}\max\left\{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2},\frac{(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F})^{2}}{t},\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|^{2}_{2}\right\}\hskip 39.74872pt\textrm{[by Lemma \ref{lem:row_norm_spectral_norm}]}\\
&\leq\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\log(n)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{t}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{t}\right)\leq\frac{2\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\log(n)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{t}\end{split}

(5.15)

where the last inequality uses that
t=\Omega(\sqrt{n})
.
Next, we bound
\mbox{\bf Var}[\mathbf{Y}]\leq{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{Y}^{2}\right]
as follows

\begin{split}{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{Y}^{2}\right]&=t\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{p_{i}}{t^{2}p_{i}^{2}}((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i})^{2}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}))^{2}-\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{2p_{i}}{p_{i}t^{2}}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{t}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i})^{2}}{p_{i}}+((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}))^{2}-\sum_{i\in[n]}2(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\right)\\
&\preceq\frac{1}{t}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{((\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i})^{2}}{p_{i}}\right)\\
&\preceq\frac{\log(n)}{t}\max\left\{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2},\frac{(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F})^{2}}{t}\right\}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i}\right)\\
&\preceq\frac{c\log(n)\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|^{2}_{2}}{t}\mathbf{I}_{n\times n}\end{split}

(5.16)

where we again use Lemma
5.5
and Theorem
5.7
. Observe,

Applying the Matrix Bernstein inequality with equations
5.15
and
5.16

\begin{split}\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{2}\geq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\right]&\leq 2n\exp{\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4}_{F}}{\frac{c\log(n)\sqrt{n}\phi_{\max}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\|^{2}_{2}}{t}+\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{n}\log(n)\phi_{\max}}{3t}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{4}_{F}}\right)}\\
&\leq 2n\exp{\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}t}{c^{\prime}\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\log(n)}\right)}\\
\end{split}

(5.17)

where the second inequality uses Theorem
5.6
, to conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta/2

\|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{2}+\epsilon/k\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq O(\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F})
.
Therefore, it suffices to set
t=\frac{c^{\prime}\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\log(n))}{\epsilon^{2}}\log(n/\delta)
, to bound the above probability by
\delta/2
. Union bounding over the error for both PCPs, and setting
\epsilon=\epsilon/k
, we can conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})-\frac{\epsilon}{k}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}\preceq(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\preceq(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\mathbf{I}

when
t=\Omega\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
.

∎

We use the spectral bound from Theorem
5.9
to obtain a row projection-cost preservation guarantee. We follow the same proof strategy as Theorem
5.7
, while requiring modified version of the scalar Chernoff bound. We do away with the head-tail split from
[
CMM17
]
,
[
MW17
]
and
[
BW18
]
and analyze the projection-cost guarantee directly. This enables us to obtain a better
\epsilon
dependence than
[
MW17
]
and
[
BW18
]
. Note, our
\epsilon
dependence matches that of
[
CMM17
]
but our row projection cost preserving sketch can be computed in sub-linear time, albeit for PSD matrices.

Theorem 5.10
.

(Row Projection-Cost Preservation.)

Given as input
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
let
\mathbf{C}
be an
n\times t
matrix as defined in Theorem
5.7
such that
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}
. Let
p=\{p_{1},p_{2}\ldots p_{n}\}
be a probability distribution over the rows of
\mathbf{C}
such that
p_{j}=\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Let
t=O\left(\frac{\phi_{max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}\log^{2}(n)}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
. Then, construct
\mathbf{R}
using
t
rows of
\mathbf{C}
and set each one to
\frac{\mathbf{C}_{i,*}}{\sqrt{tp_{i}}}
with probability
p_{i}
. With probability at least
1-c
, for any rank-
k
orthogonal projection
\mathbf{X}
,

\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}\pm O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

for a fixed constant
c
.

Proof.

Note,
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{S}\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{S}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}
, where
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{T}
are the corresponding sampling matrices.
Observe,
\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)
. Then,

\begin{split}\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}^{T}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{X}\right)\end{split}

(5.18)

where we used the fact that for any projection matrix
X=X^{2}
in addition to the cyclic property of the trace. Here, for analyzing the cross and tail terms, we observe that with probability
1-c
,
\|\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\|_{F}\leq O(1)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
and
\|\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}\|^{2}_{F}\leq O(\eta)\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Therefore,

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}+\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{X}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{X}\right)\pm O(\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.19)

Similarly,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbf{X})\right)&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{X}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{X}\right)\pm O(\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.20)

Here, we observe
\|\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\|^{2}_{F}
is an unbiased estimator for
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
and
\|\mathbf{S}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T}\|^{2}_{F}
is an unbiased estimator for
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. Using the same idea as above, we can bound the cross and tail terms by
O(\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
.
Our goal is show that Equations
5.18
and
5.20
are related up to additive error
O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. We first relate
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}\right)
. Recall,
{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{R}^{T}\mathbf{R}\right)\right]={\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{C}^{T}\mathbf{C}\right)
. Using a scalar Chernoff bound, we show that with probability at least
1-1/\textrm{poly}(n)
,
|\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Observe, for all
i\in[t]
,
\mathbf{R}_{*,i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{i^{\prime}}t}}\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},*}
for some
i^{\prime}\in[n]
. Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{p_{i^{\prime}}t}\|\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},*}\|^{2}_{2}&=\frac{\phi_{max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\epsilon^{2}}{\phi_{max}\sqrt{nk}\log(n)\log(n/\delta)\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}}\|\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},*}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{c(1+\eta)\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}}\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},*}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}}{t}\right\}\\
&\leq\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}\max\left\{\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F},\frac{\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}\right\}\\
&\leq\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.21)

where we use
\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},*}=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i^{\prime},*}+(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})_{i^{\prime},*}
,
\|(\mathbf{N}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq(\eta)\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
for all
i
and Lemma
5.8
to bound
\|(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
.
Therefore,
\frac{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}{c\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\in[0,1]
. Note,
\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
is an unbiased estimator for
\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. Using a Chernoff bound,

\begin{split}\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\geq(1+\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\right]&=\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[\frac{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\frac{\sqrt{k}\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}(1+\epsilon)\right]\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{k}\epsilon^{2}\log(n/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{10}\end{split}

(5.22)

Therefore, with probability at least
1-\delta/10
,
|\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. Note, we can then bound
\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}+2\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. Therefore,

|\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}

Recall, by equation
5.10
, with probability
\delta/10
,
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=(1\pm(\epsilon+2\sqrt{\eta}))\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}
and thus we have that
\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\leq 3\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. We can repeat the above argument to lower bound
\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
.
Therefore, with probability
1-\delta
, we have

|\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}|\leq O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.23)

Next, we relate
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{X}\right)
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
.
Observe,
\mathbf{X}
is a rank
k
projection matrix and we can represent it as
\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}
, where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and has orthonormal columns. By the cyclic property of the trace, we have

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{Z}\right)=\sum_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}

Similarly,
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}
. By Theorem
5.9
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in[k]}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\right)=\sum_{j\in[k]}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\pm\frac{\epsilon}{k}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{I}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}\right)\end{split}

(5.24)

Since
\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{*,j}\mathbf{Z}_{*,j}=1
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{Z}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{X}\right)
, we obtain

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{X}\right)-\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})\mathbf{X}\right)\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T})^{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{X}\right)+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}^{2}

(5.25)

Combining equations
5.25
,
5.23
,
5.19
and
5.20
with probability
1-c
,

\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}-O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}+O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

∎

Input
: A Matrix
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, integer
k
,
\epsilon>0
and
\phi_{\max}=\max_{j}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}/(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}

1.

Let
t=\frac{c\phi^{2}_{\max}\sqrt{n}k\log^{2}(n)}{\epsilon^{2}}
, for some constant
c
. Let
q=\{q_{1},q_{2}\ldots q_{n}\}
denote a distribution over columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that
q_{j}=\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Construct a column PCP for
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
by sampling
t
columns of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
such that each column is set to
\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{*,j}}{\sqrt{tq_{j}}}
with probability
q_{j}
. Let
\mathbf{C}
be the resulting
n\times t
matrix that satisfies the guarantee of Theorem
5.7
.

2.

Let
p=\{p_{1},p_{2}\ldots p_{n}\}
denote a distribution over rows of
\mathbf{C}
such that
p_{i}=\frac{(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)}
. Construct a row PCP for
\mathbf{C}
by sampling
t
rows of
\mathbf{C}
such that each row is set to
\frac{\mathbf{C}_{i,*}}{\sqrt{tp_{i}}}
with probability
p_{i}
. Let
\mathbf{R}
be the resulting
t\times t
matrix that satisfies the guarantee of Theorem
5.10
. Sample
\Theta(n)
entries uniformly at random from
\mathbf{A}
and rescale such that
\widetilde{v}^{2}=\Theta(\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F})
.

3.

Let
\mu=\phi_{\max}\sqrt{|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}|\cdot|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}|}

For all
i\in[t]
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\sum_{j\in[\epsilon^{3}t/k^{3}]}\mathcal{X}_{i,j}
such that
\mathcal{X}_{i,j}=(k^{3}/\epsilon^{3})\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,i^{\prime}}
, with probability
1/t
, for all
i^{\prime}\in[t]
. Here, we query the entry corresponding to
\mathbf{R}_{i,i^{\prime}}
in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and truncate it to
\mu
.
Let
\tau=\phi_{\max}^{2}n\widetilde{v}^{2}/t^{2}
. If
\mathcal{X}_{i}>\tau
, sample row
\mathbf{R}_{i,*}
with probability
1
. For the remaining rows, sample
nk/(\epsilon t)
rows uniformly at random.

4.

Run the sampling algorithm from Frieze-Kannan-Vempala
[
FKV04
]
to compute a
t\times k
matrix
\mathbf{S}
such that
\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
.
Consider the regression problem

\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{R}^{n\times k}}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}.

Sketch the problem using the leverage scores of
\mathbf{S}^{T}
, as shown in Lemma
4.4
, to obtain a sampling matrix
\mathbf{E}
with
O(\frac{k}{\epsilon})
columns. Compute

\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{R}^{n\times k}}\|\mathbf{C}\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}^{T}\mathbf{E}\|^{2}_{F}.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{S}^{T}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{T}
be such that
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
has orthonormal columns.

5.

Consider the regression problem

\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}.

Sketch the problem as above, following Lemma
4.4
to obtain a sampling matrix
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
with
O(\frac{k}{\epsilon})
rows. Compute

\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}

Output:
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{U}
,
\mathbf{N}^{T}=\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}

Full Algorithm.

Next, we describe a sublinear time and query robust algorithm for low-rank approximation of PSD matrices. We show that querying
\widetilde{O}(\phi^{2}_{\max}nk/\epsilon)
entries of
\mathbf{A}
suffices. While we assume we know
\phi_{\max}
, in practice this need not be the case. Therefore, given a budget for the total number of queries, denoted by
\beta
, we can run the algorithm by querying a
\sqrt{\beta}\times\sqrt{\beta}
submatrix (as described in Algorithm
5.2
), but correctness only holds when
\beta\geq\widetilde{\Theta}(\phi^{2}_{\max}nk/\epsilon)
. Recall, whenever we read an entry in
(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,j}
, we can truncate it to
\phi_{\max}\sqrt{|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}|\cdot|(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}|}
. We can compute these thresholds by simply reading the diagonal of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
.

We proceed by constructing column and row projection-cost preserving sketches of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, to obtain a
t\times t
matrix
\mathbf{R}
, where
t=\widetilde{O}(\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}/\epsilon^{2})
.
Instead of reading the entire matrix, we sample
\epsilon^{3}t/k^{3}
entries in each row of
\mathbf{R}
, and read these entries. Ideally we would want to estimate
\ell^{2}_{2}
norms of rows of
\mathbf{R}
to then use a result of Frieze-Kannan-Vempala
[
FKV04
]
to show that there exists an
s\times t
matrix
\mathbf{S}
such that the row space of
\mathbf{S}
contains a good rank-
k
approximation, where
s=c\phi_{\max}^{2}nk/\epsilon t
, for some constant
c
. However, we show that is it not possible to obtain accurate estimates of the row norms of each row of
\mathbf{R}
with high probability.

Instead, we describe a new sampling procedure that ends up sampling rows of
\mathbf{R}
with the same probability as Frieze-Kannan-Vempala.
Once we compute a good low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{R}
we can follow the approach of
[
CMM17
]
,
[
MW17
]
and
[
BW18
]
, where we set up two regression problems, and use fast approximate regression to compute a low rank approximation for
\mathbf{A}
. The main theorem we prove in this section is as follows:

Theorem 5.11
.

(Robust PSD LRA.)

Let
k
be an integer and
\epsilon>\eta>0
.
Given a matrix
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, where
\mathbf{A}
is PSD and
\mathbf{N}
is a corruption term such that
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\sqrt{\eta}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
and for all
i\in[n]

\|\mathbf{N}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq c\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
, for a fixed constant
c
, Algorithm
5.2
samples
\widetilde{O}\left(\phi^{2}_{\max}nk/\epsilon\right)
entries in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and computes matrices
\mathbf{M},\mathbf{N}^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that with probability at least
99/100
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

We begin with the following simple lemma for approximating the Frobenius norm :

Lemma 5.12
.

(Approximating Frobenius Norm.)

Given as input an
n\times n
matrix
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, there exists an algorithm that reads
O(\phi_{\max}^{2}n)
entries in
\mathbf{A}
and outputs an estimator
\tilde{v}
such that with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{n^{c}}
,
\tilde{v}=\Theta(\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F})
.

Proof.

There are multiple ways to see this. Observe, in Theorem
5.10
, we show that sampling
\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}n\log(n)}{\epsilon^{2}}
entries results in row projection-cost preserving sketch
\mathbf{R}
such that
\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}=(1\pm\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}
. Setting
\epsilon
to be a small constant suffices.
∎

Next, we provide intuition for why uniformly sampling columns of
\mathbf{R}
does not suffice for obtaining a sketch that spans a good low rank approximation. For simplicity, we assume there is no noise (
\eta=0
and
\phi_{\max}=1
) and show that our techniques to bound the column norms of
\mathbf{R}
