results in an estimate that is too large. We note that this lemma is not required for proving our result, and is just present for intuition.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\eta=0
.
Let
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
be a row projection-cost preserving sketch for
\mathbf{C}
as described in Theorem
5.10
.
For all
j\in[t]
, with probability at least
1-1/n^{c}
,

\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\leq O\left(\log(n)\max\left\{\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{n\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{t^{2}}\right\}\right)=O\left(\log(n)\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{t},\frac{n\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{t^{2}}\right\}\right)

where
c
is a fixed constant.

Proof.

Observe,
\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}=\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{R}_{i,j}^{2}
, where
\mathbf{R}_{i,j}^{2}=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}}\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},j}^{2}

with probability
\frac{\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}}{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}
for all
i^{\prime}\in[n]
. Then,

{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}]=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbf{C}^{2}_{i,j}=\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}
. Next, we compute the variance of
\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}
.
\mbox{\bf Var}\left[\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\right]=t\mbox{\bf Var}\left[\mathbf{R}_{i,j}\right]\leq t{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{R}^{4}_{i,j}\right]
. Then,

\begin{split}t{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{R}^{4}_{i,j}\right]=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]}\frac{1}{tp_{i^{\prime}}}\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime},j}^{4}&\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]}\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)^{2}}{t^{2}\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}}\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},j}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}\\
&\leq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)^{2}}{t^{2}}\|\mathbf{A}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}+\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{4}_{2}\hskip 28.90755pt\textrm{ [AM-GM]}\\
\end{split}

where we use
\mathbf{A}^{2}_{i,j}\leq\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}
, which follows from applying Cauchy-Schwarz to
\langle\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{i,*},\mathbf{A}^{1/2}_{j,*}\rangle
. Similarly, we bound
\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\leq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}
. Applying Bernstein’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\mbox{\bf Pr}\left[|\|\mathbf{R}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\eta\max\left\{\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2},\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right\}\right]\\
&\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\eta^{2}\max\left\{\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{4}_{2},\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}{\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}+\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{4}_{2}+\eta\max\left\{\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}}{t},\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}\right)\\
&\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\eta\max\left\{\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{4}_{2},\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}\right\}}{c^{\prime}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\right)^{2}+c^{\prime}\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{4}_{2}}\right)\\
\end{split}

where we use the AM-GM inequality on
\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t}\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}
repeatedly. Setting
\eta=\Omega(\log(n))
completes the proof. Finally, observe, for any
j\in[t]
,
\|\mathbf{C}_{*,j}\|^{2}_{2}=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)}{t\mathbf{A}_{j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\|\mathbf{A}_{*,j^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}
for some
j^{\prime}\in[n]
. We then use
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}\right)\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
.
∎

It is well-known that to recover a low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{R}
, one can sample rows of
\mathbf{R}
proportional to row norm estimates, denoted by
\mathbf{Y}_{i}

[
FKV04
]
. As shown in
[
IVWW19
]
the following two conditions are a relaxation of those required in
[
FKV04
]
, and suffice to obtain an additive error low-rank approximation :

1.

For all
i\in[t]
,
\mathcal{Y}_{i}\geq\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
.

2.

\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathcal{Y}_{i}\leq\frac{n}{t}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}

To satisfy the first condition, we need to obtain overestimates for each row. Since it is not immediately clear how to obtain overestimates for row norms of
\mathbf{R}
, a naïve approach would be to bias the estimate for each row by an upper bound on the row norm. However, by Lemma
5.13
, a row norm could be as large as
\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}/t
. Observe, we cannot afford to bias the estimator of each row,
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
, by this amount since
\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathcal{Y}_{i}\geq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
. Therefore, we would have to sample
\sqrt{n}k/\epsilon
rows of
\mathbf{R}
, resulting in us querying
\Omega(nk^{3}/\epsilon^{3})
entries in
\mathbf{A}
, even when
\eta=0
.

An alternative strategy would be to bias the estimator for each row by
n\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}/t^{2}
, as this would satisfy condition 2 above.
We can now hope to detect rows of
\mathbf{R}
that have norm larger than
n\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}/t^{2}
by sampling
\epsilon^{3}t/k^{3}
entries in each row of
\mathbf{R}
, uniformly at random. Note, this way we can construct an unbiased estimator for the
\ell^{2}_{2}
norm of each row. Ideally, we would want to show a high probability statement for concentration of our row norm estimates around the expectation. We could then union bound, and obtain concentration for all
i
simultaneously.

However, this is not possible since it may be the case that a row of
\mathbf{R}
is
\log(n)
-sparse with each entry being large in magnitude. In this case, uniformly querying the row would not observe any non-zero with good probability and thus cannot distinguish between such a row and an empty row. Instead, we settle for a weaker statement, that shows our estimate is accurate with
o(1)
probability. All subsequent statements hold for
\eta>0
.

Lemma 5.14
.

(Estimating large row norms.)

Let
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
be the row PCP output by Step 2 of Algorithm
5.2
. For all
i\in[t]
let
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\sum_{j\in[\epsilon^{3}t/k^{3}]}\mathcal{X}_{i,j}
such that
\mathcal{X}_{i,j}=\frac{k^{3}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j^{\prime}}}{\epsilon^{3}}
with probability
\frac{1}{t}
, for all
j^{\prime}\in[t]
. Then, for all
i\in[t]
,
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\left(1\pm\frac{1}{10}\right)\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
with probability at least
\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k}{\epsilon n}
.

Proof.

Observe,
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is an unbiased estimator of
\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
:

{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathcal{X}_{i}\right]=\frac{\epsilon^{3}t}{k^{3}}{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathcal{X}_{i,j}\right]=\frac{\epsilon^{3}t}{k^{3}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[t]}\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}n}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j^{\prime}}=\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}

Next, we compute the variance of
\mathcal{X}_{i}
.

\begin{split}\mbox{\bf Var}\left[\mathcal{X}_{i}\right]=\frac{\epsilon^{3}t}{k^{3}}\mbox{\bf Var}\left[\mathcal{X}_{i,j}\right]&\leq\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{4}_{i,j}\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}}{t^{2}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})^{2}_{i,j}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}}{t^{2}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}(\mathbf{A}_{i,i}\mathbf{A}_{j,j}+\mathbf{N}^{2}_{i,j})\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}\phi^{2}_{\max}}{t^{2}}+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}\phi^{2}_{\max}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}{t^{2}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}\phi^{2}_{\max}}{t^{2}}\right)\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{k}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\end{split}

(5.26)

Here, we use that
\mathbf{N}^{2}_{i,j}\leq\phi^{2}_{\max}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{i,i}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N})_{j,j}
, which follows from our truncation procedure. Further, using
t=\phi_{\max}\sqrt{n}k^{2}/\epsilon^{2}
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}+\sqrt{\eta n}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}
, we can bound

\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}\right)^{2}\phi^{2}_{\max}}{t^{2}}\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{k}\right)

Further, using the same argument as above

(5.27)

\mathcal{X}_{i,j}\leq\frac{k^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\mathbf{R}^{2}_{i,j}\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{k}\right)

Using Equations
5.26
and
5.27
in Bernstein’s inequality,

\begin{split}\Pr\left[\big|\mathcal{X}_{i}-{\bf\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{X}_{i}]\big|\geq\delta{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathcal{X}_{i}\right]\right]&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{4}_{2}}{\frac{\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{k}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{\delta\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}}{3k}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}}\right)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log^{2}(n)}{\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}\right)\end{split}

where we use that
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=\Theta(\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F})
.
Setting
\eta=\frac{1}{10}
,
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\left(1\pm\frac{1}{10}\right)\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log^{2}(n)}{\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}\right)
. Let
\xi_{i}
be the event that
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\left(1\pm\frac{1}{10}\right)\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
. Then, union bounding over
t\leq n
such events
\xi_{i}
, simultaneously for all
i
,
\xi_{i}
is true with probability at least

1-\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log(n)}{\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}\right)\geq\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log(n)}{\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}\qed

We now have two major challenges: first, the probability with which
the estimators are accurate is too small to even detect all rows with
norm larger than
\phi_{\max}^{2}n\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}/t^{2}
, and second, there is no small query certificate for when an estimator is accurate in
estimating the row norms. Therefore, we cannot even identify the rows
where we obtain an accurate estimate of their norm.

To address the first issue, we make the crucial observation that while we cannot estimate the norm of each row accurately, we can hope to sample the row with the same probability as Frieze-Kannan-Vempala
[
FKV04
]
. Recall, their algorithm samples row
\mathbf{R}_{i,*}
with probability at least
\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}/\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
, which matches the probability in Lemma
5.14
. Therefore, we can focus on designing a weaker notion of identifiability, that may potentially include extra rows.

We begin by partitioning rows of
\mathbf{R}
into two sets. Let
\mathcal{H}=\left\{i\textrm{ }\big|\textrm{ }\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\phi_{\max}^{2}n\widetilde{v}^{2}/t^{2}\right\}
be the set of heavy rows and
[t]\setminus\mathcal{H}
be the remaining rows. Since with
probability at least
1-\frac{1}{\textrm{poly}(n)}
,
\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}=\Theta(\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F})=\Theta(\widetilde{v}^{2})
,

|\mathcal{H}|=O(t^{2}/\phi_{\max}^{2}n)=O(k^{4}\log^{4}(n)/\epsilon^{4})

Observe, every row in
\mathcal{H}
can potentially satisfy the
threshold
\tau=\phi_{\max}^{2}n\widetilde{v}^{2}/t^{2}
. Therefore, even if our estimator
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is

\Theta(\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2})
for all
i\in\mathcal{H}
, we include
at most
\widetilde{O}(k^{4}/\epsilon^{4})
extra rows in
\mathbf{S}
, which is well within our budget. Observe, we can then sample a row with probability
1
whenever the corresponding estimate is larger than
\tau
. This sampling process ensures that we identify rows in
\mathcal{H}
with the right probability and also doees not query more than
O(\phi_{\max}^{2}nk/\epsilon)
entries in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
.
For all the remaining rows, we know the norm is at most

O(\phi_{\max}^{2}n\widetilde{v}^{2}/t^{2})
. We then modify the analysis of

[
FKV04
]
to show that we can handle both cases separately.

Theorem 5.15
.

(Existence
[
FKV04
]
.)
Let
\mathbf{R}
be a row projection-cost preserving sketch output by Step 2 of Algorithm
5.2
. For all
i\in[t]
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i}
be estimate for
\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
as described in Step 3 of Algorithm
5.2
. Let
\mathbf{S}
be a subset of
s=O(\phi_{\max}^{2}nk/\epsilon t)
columns of
\mathbf{R}
sampled according to distribution
r=\{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots r_{t}\}
such that
r_{i}
is the probability of sampling the
i
-th row.
Then, with probability at least
99/100
, there exists a
t\times k
matrix
\mathbf{U}
in the column span of
\mathbf{S}
such that

\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}

Proof.

We follow the proof strategy of
[
FKV04
]
and show how to directly bound the variance in our setting as opposed to reducing to the two conditions above.
Let
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{P}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{T}=\sum_{\ell\in[t]}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\ell,\ell}\mathbf{P}_{\ell,*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,*}^{T}=\sum_{\ell\in[t]}\sigma_{\ell}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}^{T}
. Recall,
\mathbf{R}_{k}=\sum_{\ell\in[k]}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}^{T}
. For
\ell\in[t]
, let
\mathbf{W}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{s}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[s]}\mathbf{Y}_{i^{\prime}}
where
\mathbf{Y}_{i^{\prime}}=\frac{\mathbf{P}_{i,\ell}}{r_{i}}\mathbf{R}_{i,*}
with probability
r_{i}
, for all
i\in[t]
. Then,

{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\right]={\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbf{Y}_{i^{\prime}}\right]=\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{\mathbf{P}_{i,\ell}}{r_{i}}\mathbf{R}_{i,*}r_{i}=\sigma_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}

(5.28)

Therefore, in expectation the span of the rows contain a good low-rank solution.
Next, we bound the variance.
Recall, here we consider the rows in
\mathcal{H}
and its complement separately. From Lemma For all
i\in\mathcal{H}
, we know that
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\Theta(\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2})
with probability at least
\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log(n)/\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}
. Since for all such
i
,
\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\tau
, the corresponding
r_{i}\geq\frac{\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}k\log(n)}{\epsilon\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}
, since every time we pass the threshold we sample the row. For all
i\notin\mathcal{H}
,
r_{i}\geq 1/t
since there can be at most
t
such
i
, and we sample each such row with uniform probability.
Once we have a lower bound on
r_{i}
in both cases, we open up the analysis of the variance bound in
[
FKV04
]
and show that our lower bounds suffice.

\begin{split}{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\mathbf{W}_{\ell}-\sigma_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{s}\left(\sum_{j\in[t]}\frac{\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}}{r_{i}}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\right)-\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\ell}}{s}&\leq\frac{1}{s}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}}\frac{\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}}{r_{i}}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}+\sum_{i\in[t]\setminus\mathcal{H}}\frac{\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}}{r_{i}}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{s}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}}\frac{k\log(n)\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}{\epsilon}+\sum_{j\in[t]\setminus\mathcal{H}}t\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}\|\mathbf{R}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{s}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}}\frac{k\log(n)\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}}{\epsilon}+\sum_{j\in[t]\setminus\mathcal{H}}\frac{n}{t}\mathbf{P}^{2}_{i,\ell}\widetilde{v}^{2}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{s}\left(\frac{k\log(n)}{\epsilon}+\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}n}{t}\right)\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.29)

Now, we can repeat the argument from
[
FKV04
]
and it suffices to set
s=\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}n}{t}+\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)\frac{k}{\epsilon}=O(\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}nk}{\epsilon t})
. For completeness, we present the rest of the proof here.
For all
\ell\in[t]
, let
\mathbf{Y}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\ell}}\mathbf{W}_{\ell}
. Let
\mathcal{V}=\textrm{span}(\mathbf{Y}_{1},\mathbf{Y}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{Y}_{k})
. Let
\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2},\ldots\mathbf{Z}_{t}
be an orthonormal basis for
\mathcal{R}^{t}
such that
\mathcal{V}=\textrm{span}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{k^{\prime}})
, where
k^{\prime}=\textrm{dim}(\mathcal{V})
. Let
\mathbf{S}=\mathbf{R}\sum_{\ell\in[k]}\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}^{T}
be the candidate low-rank approximation approximation. Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{S}\|^{2}_{F}&=\sum_{\ell\in[t]}\|(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{S})\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&=\sum_{\ell\in[k^{\prime}+1,t]}\|\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{2}\\
&=\sum_{\ell\in[k^{\prime}+1,t]}\left\|\left((\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}\right)\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}_{2}\\
&\leq\left\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}\right\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.30)

where the first equality follows from
\|\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{2}=1
, the seconds follows from
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}=0
for
\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell
, the third follows from
\langle\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}},\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\rangle=0
for all
\ell^{\prime}\leq k
and
\ell>k^{\prime}
. Let
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}=\mathbf{R}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in[k]}\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathbf{Y}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}
. Since
\mathbf{P}_{1},\mathbf{P}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{t}
forms an orthonormal basis

\begin{split}\left\|\mathbf{R}-\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\right\|^{2}_{F}&\leq\sum_{\ell\in[t]}\left\|\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\left(\mathbf{R}-\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}\\
&=\sum_{\ell\in[k]}\left\|\sigma_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}-\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}_{2}+\sum_{\ell\in[k+1,t]}\sigma_{\ell}^{2}\end{split}

(5.31)

Taking expectations on both sides of equation
5.31
, we have

\begin{split}{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\mathbf{R}-\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]&\leq{\bf\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{\ell\in[k]}\left\|\sigma_{\ell}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}-\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\right\|^{2}_{2}\right]+\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\leq\frac{k}{s}\left(\frac{k\log(n)}{\epsilon}+\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}n}{t}\right)\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}+\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.32)

Since
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}
is a rank
k
matrix and
\mathbf{R}_{k}
is the best rank
k
approximation to
\mathbf{R}
,
\|\mathbf{R}-\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
is a non-negative random variable. Thus, using Markov’s inequality and Equation
5.30
,

\Pr\left[\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{S}\|^{2}_{F}-\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\frac{100nk}{st}\|\mathbf{R}\|^{2}_{F}\right]\leq\frac{1}{100}

Therefore, it suffices to sample
s=O\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}nk}{\epsilon t}\right)
columns, read all of them and compute a low rank approximation for
\mathbf{R}
with probability at least
\frac{99}{100}
. Observe, the total entries read by this algorithm is
O\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}nk}{\epsilon t}\cdot t\right)=O\left(\frac{\phi_{\max}^{2}nk}{\epsilon}\right)
.
∎

It remains to show that we can now recover a low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{A}
, in factored form, from the low-rank approximation for
\mathbf{R}
. Here, we follow the approach of
[
CMM17
]
,
[
MW17
]
and
[
BW18
]
, where we set up two regression problems, and use the sketch and solve paradigm to compute an approximate solution. We use the following Lemma from
[
BW18
]
that relates a good low-rank approximation of an additive error project-cost preserving sketch to a low-rank approximation of the original matrix. A similar guarantee for relative error appears in
[
CMM17
]
and
[
MW17
]
.

Lemma 5.16
.

(Lemma 4.4 in
[
BW18
]
.)

Let
\mathbf{C}
be a column PCP for
\mathbf{A}
satisfying the guarantee of Theorem
5.7
. Let
\mathbf{P}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\textrm{rank}(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}

and
\mathbf{P}^{*}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\textrm{rank}(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
.
Then, for any rank
k
projection matrix
\mathbf{P}
such that
\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{P}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}
, with probability at least
99/100
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{P}^{*}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

A similar guarantee holds for a row PCP of
\mathbf{A}
.

Note, while
\mathbf{R}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{T}
is an approximate rank-
k
solution for
\mathbf{R}
, it does not have the same dimensions as
\mathbf{A}
. If we do not consider running time, we could construct a low-rank approximation to
\mathbf{A}
as follows: since projecting
\mathbf{R}
onto
\mathbf{S}^{T}
is approximately optimal, it follows from Lemma
5.16
that with probability
99/100
,

\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\pm(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.33)

Let
\mathbf{C}_{k}=\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\prime T}
be such that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
has orthonormal columns. Then,
\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime T}\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}
and by Lemma
5.16
it follows that with probability
98/100
,
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime T}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
. However, even approximately computing a column space
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
for
\mathbf{C}_{k}
using an input-sparsity time algorithm, such as
[
CW13
]
, could require
\Omega\left(nt\right)
queries. To get around this issue, we observe that an approximate solution for
\mathbf{R}
lies in the row space of
\mathbf{S}^{T}
and therefore, an approximately optimal solution for
\mathbf{C}
lies in the row space of
\mathbf{S}^{T}
. We then set up the following regression problem:

\min_{\textrm{rank}(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.34)

Note, this regression problem is still too large to be solved in sublinear time. Therefore, we sketch it by sampling columns of
\mathbf{C}
to set up a smaller regression problem. Observe, since
\mathbf{S}
has orthonormal columns, the leverage scores are simply
\ell^{2}_{2}
norms of rows of
\mathbf{S}
. Now, using Lemma
4.4
, approximately solving this regression problem requires sampling
\Omega(k/\epsilon)
rows of
\mathbf{C}
, which in turn requires
\Omega(\frac{nk}{\epsilon})
queries to
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
.
Note, the above theorem applied to Equation
5.34
can take
O\left(nk+\textrm{poly}(k,\epsilon^{-1})\right)
time and thus is a lower order term.
Since
\mathbf{S}^{T}
has orthonomal rows, the leverage scores are precomputed. With probability at least
99/100
, we can compute
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{C}\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}^{T}\mathbf{E}\|^{2}_{F}
, where
\mathbf{E}
is a leverage score sketching matrix with
O\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)
columns, as shown in Lemma
4.4
, and thus requires
O\left(\frac{nk}{\epsilon}\right)
queries to
\mathbf{A}
.
Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}&\leq(1+\epsilon)\min_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\leq(1+\epsilon)\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{S}^{T}\|^{2}_{F}\\
&=\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\pm(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.35)

where the last two inequalities follow from equation
5.33
.
Let
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{S}^{T}=\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\prime T}
be such that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
has orthonormal columns. Then, the column space of
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
contains an approximately optimal solution for
\mathbf{A}
, since
\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\prime T}\|^{2}_{F}=\|\mathbf{C}-\mathbf{C}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}\pm\epsilon\|\mathbf{C}\|^{2}_{F}
and
\mathbf{C}
is a column PCP for
\mathbf{A}
. It follows from Lemma
5.16
that with probability at least
98/100
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime T}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|_{F}

(5.36)

Therefore, there exists a good solution for
\mathbf{A}
in the column space of
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
. Since we cannot compute this explicitly, we set up the following regression problem:

\min_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}

(5.37)

Again, we sketch the regression problem above by sampling columns of
\mathbf{A}
and apply Lemma
4.4
. We can then compute
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathrm{argmin}_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}
with probability at least
99/100
, where
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
is a sketching matrix with
\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)
rows and
O\left(\frac{nk}{\epsilon}\right)
queries to
\mathbf{A}
. Then,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}\|^{2}_{F}&\leq(1+\epsilon)\min_{\mathbf{X}}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\leq(1+\epsilon)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime T}\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\\
&\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+O(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}\end{split}

(5.38)

where the second inequality follows from
\mathbf{X}
being the minimizer and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime T}\mathbf{A}
being some other matrix, and the last inequality follows from equation
5.36
.
Recall,
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
is an
n\times k
matrix and the time taken to solve the regression problem is
O\left(nk+\textrm{poly}(k,\epsilon^{-1})\right)
.

Therefore, we observe that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}
suffices and we output it in factored form by setting
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{A}}^{T}
. Union bounding over the probabilistic events, and rescaling
\epsilon
, with probability at least
9/10
, Algorithm
5.2
outputs
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbf{R}^{n\times k}
and
\mathbf{N}^{T}\in\mathbf{R}^{n\times k}
such that the total number of entries queried in
\mathbf{A}
are
\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\phi^{2}_{\max}nk}{\epsilon}\right)
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
5.11
.

Correlation Matrices.

We introduce low-rank approximation of correlation Matrices, a special case of PSD matrices
where the diagonal is all
1
s.
Correlation matrices are well studied in numerical linear algebra, statistics and finance since an important statistic of
n
random variables
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots X_{n}
is given by computing the pairwise correlation coefficient,
\textrm{corr}(X_{i},X_{j})=\textrm{cov}(X_{i},X_{j})/\sqrt{\textrm{var}(X_{i})\cdot\textrm{var}(X_{j})}
. A natural matrix representation of correlation coefficients results in a
n\times n
correlation matrix
\mathbf{A}
such that
\mathbf{A}_{i,j}=\textrm{corr}(X_{i},X_{j})
.

Definition 5.17
.

(Correlation Matrices.)

\mathbf{A}
is an
n\times n
correlation matrix if
\mathbf{A}
is PSD and
\mathbf{A}_{i,i}=1
, for all
i\in[n]
.

Often, in practice the correlation matrices obtained are close to being PSD, but corrupted by noise in the form of missing or asynchronous observations, stress testing or aggregation. Here the goal is to query few entries of the corrupted matrix and recover a rank-
k
matrix close to the underlying correlation matrix, assuming that the underlying matrix is also close to low rank to begin with.

Here we observe that since correlation matrices have all diagonal entries equal to
1
, we can compute
\phi_{\max}
by simply reading the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
. However, we can do even better since we can discard the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
.
The main insight here is that for correlation matrices, our algorithm simply uniformly samples columns and rows to construct our row and column PCPs, since we know what the true diagonals should be.
In this case, no matter what the adversary does to the diagonal,
\phi_{\max}=1
and we obtain a
\widetilde{O}(nk/\epsilon)
query algorithm.

Corollary 5.18
.

(Robust LRA for Correlation Matrices.)

Let
k
be an integer and
1>\epsilon>\eta>0
. Given
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
, where
\mathbf{A}
is a correlation matrix and
\mathbf{N}
is a corruption term such that
\|\mathbf{N}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\eta\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}
and for all
i\in[n]

\|\mathbf{N}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq c\|\mathbf{A}_{i,*}\|^{2}_{2}
for a fixed constant
c
, there exists an algorithm that samples
\widetilde{O}\left(nk/\epsilon\right)
entries in
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{N}
and with probability at least
99/100
, computes a rank
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+(\epsilon+\sqrt{\eta})\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

Note, the sample complexity of this algorithm is optimal, since there is an
\Omega(nk/\epsilon)
query lower bound for additive-error low-rank approximation of correlation matrices, even when there is no corruption (see Corollary
5.20
).

Additive-Error PSD Low-Rank Approximation.

In the limit where
\eta=0
,
\phi_{max}=1
, and we obtain an algorithm with query complexity
\widetilde{O}(nk/\epsilon)
. While this guarantee is already implied by our algorithm for
relative-error
low-rank approximation, our additive-error algorithm is simpler to implement, since the sampling probabilities can be computed
exactly
by simply reading the diagonal.

Corollary 5.19
.

(Sample-Optimal Additive-Error LRA.)
Given a PSD matrix
\mathbf{A}
, rank parameter
k
, and
\epsilon>0
, there exists an algorithm that samples
\widetilde{O}(nk/\epsilon)
entries in
\mathbf{A}
and outputs a rank-
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that with probability at least
99/100
,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

We show a matching lower bound on the query complexity of additive-error low-rank approximation of PSD matrices. Here, we simply observe that the lower bound construction introduced by
[
MW17
]
of
\Omega\left(\frac{nk}{\epsilon}\right)
also holds for additive error. As a consequence our algorithm is optimal in the setting where there is no corruption.

Corollary 5.20
.

(Correlation Matrix Lower Bound, Theorem 13
[
MW17
]
.)

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a PSD matrix,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\epsilon>0
be such that
\frac{nk}{\epsilon}=o(n^{2})
. Any randomized algorithm,
\mathcal{A}
, that with probability at least
2/3
, computes a rank
k
matrix
\mathbf{B}
such that

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}+\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}

must read
\Omega\left(\frac{nk}{\epsilon}\right)
entries of
\mathbf{A}
on some input, possibly adaptively, in expectation.

Proof.

We observe that in the lower bound construction of
[
MW17
]
, the matrix
\mathbf{A}
is binary, with all
1
s on a the diagonal, and
k
off-diagonal blocks of all
1
s, each of size
\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon n}{k}}\times\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon n}{k}}
. Therefore,
\mathbf{A}
is a correlation matrix and
\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=(1+2\epsilon)n
. Further, the optimal rank-
k
cost,
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}_{k}\|^{2}_{F}=\Theta(n)
. To compute an additive-error approximation, any algorithm must caputure
\epsilon\|\mathbf{A}\|^{2}_{F}=\epsilon n
mass among the off-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{A}
. Note, the remaining proof is identical to Theorem 13 in
[
MW17
]
.
∎
